. Suppose the bound

(3.51)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{B}F(x)[\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{k}]\mathrm{d}{\mathfrak{m}(x)}\Big|\leq C_{\mathfrak{m}}\mu(B)\|\mathbbm{1}_{B}F\|_{\mathbb{S}}\qquad\forall B\in\mathbb{B},

\displaystyle\forall F\in L^{1}_{\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}}(E)\otimes^{k}{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}}^{\prime}

holds with some constant
C_{\mathfrak{m}}>0
and

(3.52)

\mu(A)=0\hskip 1.69998pt\Rightarrow\mathfrak{m}(A)=0\qquad\forall A\in\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{X}).

Then we have

(3.53)

|\int_{\mathbb{X}}F(x)[\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{k}]\hskip 1.69998pt\mathrm{d}{\mathfrak{m}(x)}|\lesssim C_{\mathfrak{m}}\|F\|_{L^{1}_{\mu}\mathbb{S}}\qquad\forall F\in L^{1}_{\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}}(E)\otimes^{k}{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}}^{\prime}.

Proof.

If
\|F\|_{L^{1}_{\mu}\mathbb{S}}\leq 1
and
F\in L^{1}_{\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{m}}(E)\otimes^{k}{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}}^{\prime}
we enact the atomic decomposition of
F
as per

Proposition

3.2
to obtain

(3.54)

F=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}F_{k}

with

(3.55)

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu(\|F_{k}\|_{\mathbb{S}}>0)\|F_{k}\|_{\mathbb{S}}\lesssim\|F\|_{L^{1}_{\mu}\mathbb{S}}.

We claim that

(3.56)

\Big|\int_{\mathbb{X}}F_{k}(x)[\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{k}]\mathrm{d}{\mathfrak{m}(x)}\Big|\leq C_{\mathfrak{m}}\mu(\|F_{k}\|_{\mathbb{S}}>0)\|F_{k}\|_{\mathbb{S}}.

This would allow us to conclude by summing the above estimate over
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and using that
F_{k}
are supported on disjoint sets. The assumptions on

\mu
,
\mathfrak{m}
, and on the integrability of
F
justify the convergence of
appropriate series. To prove the claimed bound on
F_{k}
we choose
E_{k}\in\mathbb{B}^{\cup}
such that
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{X}\setminus E_{k}}F_{k}=0
and
\mu(E_{k})\approx\mu\left(\|F_{k}\|_{\mathbb{S}}>0\right)
. We then cover
E_{k}

optimally with sets from
\mathbb{B}
by selecting
B_{k,n}\in\mathbb{B}
such that
E_{k}\subset\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{N}}B_{k,n}
with
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mu(B_{k,n})\lesssim\mu(E_{k})
. It holds that

(3.57)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{X}}F_{k}(x)[\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{k}]\mathrm{d}{\mathfrak{m}(x)}\mathfrak{m}(x)\right|\leq\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\left|\int_{\Delta B_{k,n}}F_{k}(x)[\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{k}]\mathrm{d}{\mathfrak{m}(x)}\mathfrak{m}(x)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{\mathfrak{m}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{N}}\mu(\Delta B_{k,n})\left\|\mathbbm{1}_{\Delta B_{k,n}}F_{k}\right\|_{\mathbb{S}}\lesssim\mu\left(\|F_{k}\|_{\mathbb{S}}>0\right)\|F_{k}\|_{\mathbb{S}}.

We used the monotonicity property (
3.7
) in the above
inequalities, and (
3.3
) to find the sets
B_{k,n}\in\mathbb{B}
. Summing the above inequality over
k
yields the claim.
∎

3.2.
The time-frequency-scale
space

In this section we discuss the outer

Lebesgue space structure we will be using
in this paper. First we introduce trees: distinguished subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}

that represent frequency- and space-localized portions of
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}
,
interpreted as the group of symmetries (as per (
1.7
)). Next we
introduce the multiple sizes that will come into play in our argument. We
first present all of them succinctly to allow for efficient consultation; we
postpone the discussion about the motivation and approximate meaning of these
sizes to the end of the corresponding subsection.

3.2.1.
The
\sigma
-generating collection: trees

We begin our construction of the outer Lebesgue space on
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}
by
introducing the family of distinguished sets,
trees
, efficiently
describe the local geometry of the time-frequency-scale space and play the
role of a
\sigma
-generating collection.

Definition 3.6

(Trees)
.

A tree
T_{\Theta}(\xi_{T},x_{T},s_{T})
with top
(\xi_{T},x_{T},s_{T})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}
is the set given by

(3.58)

\displaystyle T_{\Theta}(\xi_{T},x_{T},s_{T})\coloneqq\pi_{(\xi_{T},x_{T},s_{T})}(\mathfrak{T}_{\Theta}),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}_{\Theta}\coloneqq\left\{(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)\in\Theta\times B_{1}\times(0,1):\sigma<1-|\zeta|\right\},

\displaystyle\pi_{(\xi,x,s)}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)\coloneqq(\xi+\theta(s\sigma)^{-1},x+s\zeta,s\sigma).

The set
\Theta\subset\mathbb{R}
can be any Borel, that mainly is assumed to be
an open interval
\Theta\supset B_{4}
.

There is a one-to-one correspondence between the datum
\Theta
,
(\xi_{T},x_{T},s_{T})
and a tree
T
. Given a tree
T
, we call
\mathfrak{T}_{\Theta}
its model tree,

\Theta
is its model frequency band, the
\xi_{T}
is the tree’s frequency,

B_{s_{T}}(x_{T})
is its spatial interval,
\xi_{T}+s_{T}^{-1}\Theta
is its
frequency band, and
\pi_{T}\coloneqq\pi_{(\xi_{T},x_{T},s_{T})}
is its local
coordinate map. We denote by
\mathbb{T}_{\Theta}
the collection of all
trees
T=T_{\Theta}(\xi_{T},x_{T},s_{T})
. By
\mathbb{T}_{\Theta}^{\cup}
we denote
countable unions of trees.

We can now use trees to generate an outer measures
\mu_{\Theta}^{\mathfrak{p}}
on

\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}
that allow us to measure how large a collection of trees is.

Definition 3.7

(Forest outer measure)
.

Given a countable collection
\mathcal{T}\subset\mathbb{T}_{\Theta}
we denote its counting function by

(3.59)

\displaystyle N_{\mathcal{T}}(z)\coloneqq\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{s_{T}}(x_{T})}(z).

The outer measures
\mu_{\Theta}^{\mathfrak{p}}
of a set
E\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}
is
defined in terms of the counting function of a covering by trees:

(3.60)

\mu_{\Theta}^{\mathfrak{p}}(E)\coloneqq\inf\Big\{\|N_{\mathcal{T}}(z)\|_{L^{\mathfrak{p}}_{\mathrm{d}z}(\mathbb{R})}:\mathcal{T}\subset\mathbb{T}_{\Theta},\;E\subset\bigcup_{T\in\mathcal{T}}T\Big\},\qquad\mathfrak{p}\in[1,+\infty].

We are principally interested in
\mathfrak{p}\in\{1,\infty\}
.

Any tree
T_{\Theta}(\xi_{T},x_{T},s_{T})
can be obtained from
T_{\Theta}(0,0,1)
as follows.

(3.61)

T_{\Theta}(\xi_{T},x_{T},s_{T})=\{(\xi_{T}+s_{T}^{-1}\eta,x_{T}+s_{T}y,s_{T}t):(\eta,y,t)\in T_{\Theta}(0,0,1)\}.

This is in agreement with the understanding that trees represent localized
subsets of
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}
seen as a group of symmetries. As a matter of fact, if

(\eta,y,t)\in T_{\Theta}(\xi_{T},x_{T},s_{T})
it holds that the spatial
support of
\mathrm{Tr}_{y}\mathrm{Mod}_{\eta}\mathrm{Dil}_{t}\varphi(z)
is essentially contained in

B_{s_{T}}(x_{T})
; more precisely

(3.62)

\left|\mathrm{Tr}_{y}\mathrm{Mod}_{\eta}\mathrm{Dil}_{t}\varphi(z)\right|\lesssim\left\langle\frac{z-x_{T}}{s_{T}}\right\rangle^{-N}\|\varphi\|_{\Phi^{N}_{\mathfrak{r}}}.

Furthermore, localization in Fourier space can be seen from the fact that

(3.63)

\mathrm{dist}\left(\mathrm{spt}\left(\widehat{\mathrm{Tr}_{y}\mathrm{Mod}_{\eta}\mathrm{Dil}_{t}\varphi}\right),\xi_{T}\right)\lesssim\mathrm{diam}\left(\mathrm{spt}\left(\widehat{\mathrm{Tr}_{y}\mathrm{Mod}_{\eta}\mathrm{Dil}_{t}\varphi}\right)\right)

for any
(\eta,y,t)\in T_{\Theta}(\xi_{T},x_{T},s_{T})
. Note however that
trees are not scale-localized e.g. if
(\eta,y,t)\in T_{\Theta}(\xi_{T},x_{T},s_{T})
then
(\eta,y,t^{\prime})\in T_{\Theta}(\xi_{T},x_{T},s_{T})
for all
t^{\prime}<t
. Trees respects the symmetry property of the map
f\mapsto\mathrm{Emb}[f]
. It
follows from (
1.12
) that

(3.64)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Emb}[f](\eta,y,t)[\varphi]

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\xi(y-x)}\mathrm{Emb}\left[s\mathrm{Dil}_{s^{-1}}\mathrm{Mod}_{-\xi}\mathrm{Tr}_{-x}f\right]\Bigl(s(\eta-\xi),\frac{y-x}{s},\frac{t}{s}\Bigr)[\varphi]

Identities (
3.61
) and (
3.64
) together give

(3.65)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Emb}[f]\circ\pi_{T}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\xi_{T}s_{T}\zeta}\mathrm{Emb}\left[s_{T}\mathrm{Dil}_{s_{T}^{-1}}\mathrm{Mod}_{-\xi_{T}}\mathrm{Tr}_{-x_{T}}f\right]\circ\pi_{(0,0,1)}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma).

3.2.2.
Sizes

The second ingredient of an outer Lebesgue spaces are sizes. To construct
sizes, we first introduce
local sizes
measure how “large” a
function is on any fixed tree
T\in\mathbb{T}_{\Theta}
. Formally, a

local size
is a collection
\left(\mathbb{S}(T)\right)_{T\in\mathbb{T}_{\Theta}}
indexed by
T\in\mathbb{T}_{\Theta}
of quasi-norms on
\mathscr{R}(T)\otimes^{k}{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}}^{\prime}
for some fixed
k\in\mathbb{N}
; we require
that the quasi-triangle constants
{\operatorname{Tri}_{{}_{\mathbb{S}(T)}}}
be uniform i.e.
that there exist
\operatorname{Tri}_{\mathbb{S}}\geq 1
such that

(3.66)

\|F_{1}+F_{2}\|_{\mathbb{S}(T)}\leq\operatorname{Tri}_{\mathbb{S}}\left(\|F_{1}\|_{\mathbb{S}(T)}+\|F_{1}\|_{\mathbb{S}(T)}\right)\qquad\forall T\in\mathbb{T}_{\Theta},\;\forall F_{1},F_{2}\in\mathscr{R}(T)\otimes^{k}{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}}^{\prime}.

Local sizes generate a size by setting

(3.67)

\|F\|_{\mathbb{S}}\coloneqq\sup_{T\in\mathbb{T}_{\Theta}}\sup_{W^{-}\in\mathbb{T}^{\cup}_{\Theta}}\|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}\setminus W^{-}}F\|_{\mathbb{S}(T)}

where we identify
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}\setminus W^{-}}F
appearing above with its
restriction to
T
. The upper bound over
W^{-}
guarantees that monotonicity
(
3.7
) holds.

We will now introduce several (local) sizes that will each control relevant
properties of functions
F\in\mathscr{R}(T){\otimes\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}}^{\prime}
.
These sizes are combined to obtain the size
\mathbb{F}^{u}_{\Theta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}
in
(
1.29
). Most of the sizes below depend on
the datum
N,\mathfrak{r}
and
\Theta
that we track but do not explicitly discuss.

Definition 3.8

(Lebesgue size)
.

Let
(u,v)\in[1,\infty]^{2}
. We define the local
sizes
\mathbb{L}{}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}
as follows. For
F\in\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})
and
T\in\mathbb{T}_{\Theta}
set

(3.68)

\displaystyle\|F\|_{\mathbb{L}{}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}(T)}\coloneqq{\sup_{G}}\left|\frac{1}{|\Theta|s_{T}}\int_{T}G(\eta,y,t)\mathrm{d}F(\eta,y,t)\right|

where the upper bound gets taken over all
G\in C^{\infty}_{c}(T)
with
\|G\circ\pi_{T}\|_{L^{u^{\prime}}_{\frac{\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}\zeta}{|\Theta|}}L^{v^{\prime}}_{\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma}{\sigma}}}\leq 1
, where
u^{\prime}=\frac{u}{u-1},v^{\prime}=\frac{v}{v-1}
and

(3.69)

\displaystyle\|G\circ\pi_{T}\|_{L^{u^{\prime}}_{\frac{\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}\zeta}{|\Theta|}}L^{v^{\prime}}_{\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma}{\sigma}}}:=\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|G\circ\pi_{T}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)|^{v^{\prime}}\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma}{\sigma}\Big)^{\frac{u^{\prime}}{v^{\prime}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}\zeta}{|\Theta|}\Big)^{\frac{1}{u^{\prime}}}.

The integral in (
3.68
) should be interpreted as the
integration of the test function
G(\eta,y,t)
against the measure
F
.
For
F\in\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})\otimes^{k}{{\Phi^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{r}}}^{\prime}}

we define the local sizes

(3.70)

\|F\|_{\mathbb{L}{}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}{:=\sup_{\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{k}}}\|F[\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{k}]\|_{\mathbb{L}{}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}(T)}

with the upper bound being taken over all wave packets
\varphi_{1},\ldots,\varphi_{k}\in\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}
with
\|\varphi_{j}\|_{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}}\leq 1
,

j\in\{1,\ldots,k\}
.

In (
3.68
) we defined the size by duality because we
need to deal with the eventuality that
F
is a Radon measure and is not given
by a locally integrable function w.r.t to the measure
\mathrm{d}\eta\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}t
.
If
F\in L^{1}_{\operatorname{loc}}
then it simply holds that

(3.71)

\|F\|_{\mathbb{L}{}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}}=\left\|\left(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{T}_{\Theta}}F\circ\pi_{T}\right)(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)\right\|_{L^{u}_{\frac{\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}\zeta}{|\Theta|}}L^{v}_{\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma}{\sigma}}}.

Furthermore, if
u\neq\{1,\infty\}
and
v\neq\{1,\infty\}
then
\|F\|_{\mathbb{L}{}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}}<\infty
only if
F\in L^{1}_{\operatorname{loc}}
.
However, we will come across functions of the form
F(\eta,y,t)t\delta\left(t-\mathfrak{b}(\eta,y)\right)\mathrm{d}\eta\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}t
for some
F\in L^{\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})
and for some continuous
function
\mathfrak{b}:\mathbb{R}^{2}\rightarrow[0,+\infty]
. Integrating a test
function
G
against such a Radon measure amounts to computing the integral

(3.72)

G\mapsto\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}G\left(\eta,y,\mathfrak{b}(\eta,y)\right)F\left(\eta,y,\mathfrak{b}(\eta,y)\right)\mathfrak{b}(\eta,y)\mathrm{d}\eta\mathrm{d}y.

Definition 3.9

(Lacunary size)
.

Let
(u,v)\in[1,\infty]^{2}
; we define the local
sizes
\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}
as follows. For
F\in\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}){{\otimes\Phi^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{r}}}^{\prime}}
and
T\in\mathbb{T}_{\Theta}

set

(3.73)

\|F(\eta,y,t)\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}:=\left\|\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(t(\eta-\xi_{T}))F(\eta,y,t)\right\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}.

The linear operator
\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(\theta)
, defined on
F\in\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})\otimes\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})^{\prime}
is given by

(3.74)

\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(\theta)F(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)[\varphi]\coloneqq F(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)[(-d_{z}+2\pi i\theta)\varphi(z)]

where
z
in
\mathrm{RHS}{{\eqref{eq:space-boost}}}
plays the role of a dummy
variable.

Definition 3.10

(Defect size)
.

Let
(u,v)\in[1,\infty]^{2}
; we define the
space defect local sizes
{\mathbb{L}_{\Theta}^{(u,v)}}\Box_{\zeta,\mathfrak{r}}^{N}
and the scale defect local sizes
\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\Box_{\sigma,\mathfrak{r}}^{N}
as follows. For
F\in\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}){{\otimes\Phi^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{r}}}^{\prime}}
and
T\in\mathbb{T}_{\Theta}
set

(3.75)

\displaystyle\|F\|_{{\mathbb{L}_{\Theta}^{(u,v)}}\Box^{N}_{\zeta,\mathfrak{r}}(T)}:=\left\|\left(\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(t(\eta-\xi_{T}))-t\mathrm{d}_{y}+2\pi i\xi_{T}\right)F(\eta,y,t)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{\Theta}^{(u,v)}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)},

\displaystyle\|F\|_{\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\Box^{N}_{\sigma,\mathfrak{r}}(T)}:=\left\|\left(\mathfrak{d}_{\sigma}(t(\eta-\xi_{T}))-t\mathrm{d}_{t}+(\eta-\xi_{T})\mathrm{d}_{\eta}\right)F(\eta,y,t)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{\Theta}^{(u,v)}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}.

The operator
\mathfrak{d}_{\sigma}(\theta)
, defined on
\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}){\otimes\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}}^{\prime}
, is given by

(3.76)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}_{\sigma}(\theta)F(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)[\varphi]:=F(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)[(-d_{z}+2\pi i\theta)(z\varphi(z))].

The derivatives appearing in (
3.75
) are intended in the
distributional sense.

We combine the two defect sizes and use the shorthand

(3.77)

{\mathbb{L}_{\Theta}^{(u,v)}}\Box^{N}_{\mathfrak{r}}(T)\coloneqq{\mathbb{L}_{\Theta}^{(u,v)}}\Box^{N}_{\zeta,\mathfrak{r}}(T)+{\mathbb{L}_{\Theta}^{(u,v)}}\Box^{N}_{\sigma,\mathfrak{r}}(T).

We introduce an additional size that represents the maximal truncation of a
localized singular integral operator. This size has not been studied in prior
works that use outer Lebesgue spaces in the context of time frequency
analysis. Controlling this size for functions
F=\mathrm{Emb}[f]
is one of the
main objectives of
Section

6.1
.

Definition 3.11

(SIO truncation size)
.

Let
u\in[1,\infty)
; we define the SIO
truncation local sizes
\mathbb{J}_{\Theta}^{u}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}
as follows. For
F\in\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}){{\otimes\Phi^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{r}}}^{\prime}}
and
T\in\mathbb{T}_{\Theta}
set

(3.78)

\displaystyle\|F\|_{\mathbb{J}_{\Theta}^{u}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}\coloneqq{\sup_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{aligned} &\varphi,G\end{aligned}$}}}}\left|\frac{1}{s_{T}|\Theta|}\int_{T}\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(t(\eta-\xi_{T}))F(\eta,y,t)G(\eta,y,t)\mathrm{d}\eta\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}t\right|

where the upper bound gets taken over all
\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}

with
\|\varphi\|_{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}}\leq 1
and over all
G\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})
such that

(3.79)

\|\sigma d_{\sigma}G\circ\pi_{T}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)\|_{L^{u^{\prime}}_{\frac{\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}\zeta}{|\Theta|}}L^{\infty}_{\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma}{\sigma}}}\leq 1.

With these definitions in hand we have all the elements necessary to
understand (
1.29
) that defines the size

\mathbb{F}^{u}_{\Theta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}
. The single summands in
(
1.29
) are related to the local sizes
above through (
3.67
).

3.2.3.
Sizes: some technical remarks

All the sizes above are regular in the sense of (
3.8
). On
a technical level, this allows us to prove most of our statements under the
assumptions that the functions
F\in\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}){\otimes\Phi^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{r}}}^{\prime}
in play are compactly supported.

Remark 3.12
.

There exists two sequences of forests
W_{n}^{+}\in\mathbb{T}_{\Theta}^{\cup}
and
W_{n}^{-}\in\mathbb{T}_{\Theta}^{\cup}
such that
W^{+}_{n}

is increasing,
W^{-}_{n}
is decreasing, and
\mathbb{K}_{n}\coloneqq W^{+}_{n}\setminus W^{-}_{n}
are compact, increasing, and satisfy
\bigcup_{n}\mathbb{K}_{n}=\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}
.

We simplify dealing with the dependence on
\varphi\in\Phi^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{r}}

appearing in the definition of all sizes above using the following wave packet
decomposition result.

Lemma 3.13
.

For any
0<N^{\prime}<N
there exists a
sequence of test functions
\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}\in\Phi_{2\mathfrak{r}}^{N^{\prime}}
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}
satisfying
\|\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}\|_{\Phi^{N^{\prime}}_{2\mathfrak{r}}}\lesssim 1
such
that the following decomposition holds. For any
\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}

there exists a sequence
a_{k}\in\mathbb{C}
such that

(3.80)

\varphi(z)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}a_{k}\widetilde{\varphi}_{k},

and
|a_{k}|\lesssim\langle k\rangle^{-(N-N^{\prime})}\|\varphi\|_{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}}.
In particular, the above sum converges absolutely in

\Phi^{N^{\prime}}_{2\mathfrak{r}}
if
N>N^{\prime}+1
.

Proof.

The proof is based on the Fourier inversion formula. Fix
0<\varepsilon<1
and set

(3.81)

\displaystyle\widehat{\widetilde{\varphi}}\left(\widehat{z}\right)\coloneqq\left(\left|\widehat{z}\right|-2\mathfrak{r}\right)^{(N^{\prime}+\varepsilon)}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{2\mathfrak{r}}}\left(\widehat{z}\right),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}(z)\coloneqq\langle k\rangle^{-N^{\prime}}\widetilde{\varphi}\left(z+\frac{k}{4\mathfrak{r}}\right)

so that
\|\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}(z)\|_{\Phi^{N^{\prime}}_{2\mathfrak{r}}}\lesssim 1
. Let

(3.82)

\displaystyle a_{k}\coloneqq\frac{\langle k\rangle^{N^{\prime}}}{\left(4\mathfrak{r}\right)^{2}}\int_{B_{2\mathfrak{r}}}e^{-2\pi i\frac{k}{4\mathfrak{r}}\widehat{w}}\widehat{\varphi}\left(\widehat{w}\right)\left(\left|\widehat{w}\right|-2\mathfrak{r}\right)^{-(N^{\prime}+\varepsilon)}\mathrm{d}\widehat{w}.

Since
\widehat{\varphi}
is supported on
B_{\mathfrak{r}}
it holds that
\left\|\widehat{\varphi}\left(\widehat{w}\right)\left(\left|\widehat{w}\right|-2\mathfrak{r}\right)^{-(N^{\prime}+\varepsilon)}\right\|_{C^{N}}\approx\left\|\widehat{\varphi}\left(\widehat{w}\right)\right\|_{C^{N}}
so
|a_{k}|\lesssim\langle k\rangle^{-(N-N^{\prime})}\|\varphi\|_{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}}
. The Fourier inversion
formula gives us that

(3.83)

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}a_{k}\langle k\rangle^{-N^{\prime}}e^{2\pi i\frac{k}{4\mathfrak{r}}\widehat{z}}=\widehat{\varphi}\left(\widehat{z}\right)\left(\left|\widehat{z}\right|-2\mathfrak{r}\right)^{-(N^{\prime}+\varepsilon)}

with the series converging uniformly. It follows that

(3.84)

\displaystyle\widehat{\varphi}\left(\widehat{z}\right)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}a_{k}\langle k\rangle^{-N^{\prime}}e^{2\pi i\frac{k}{4\mathfrak{r}}\widehat{z}}\widehat{\widetilde{\varphi}}\left(\widehat{z}\right)

i.e.

(3.85)

\varphi(z)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}a_{k}\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}(z)

as required.
∎

As a consequence one can compare
F\in\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}){\otimes\Phi^{\infty}_{2\mathfrak{r}}}^{\prime}
with scalar functions in the following sense.

Corollary 3.14
.

Let
(\eta,y,t)\mapsto\varphi_{(\eta,y,t)}\in\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}
be a measurable function with
\|\varphi_{(\eta,y,t)}\|_{\Phi^{N}_{\mathfrak{r}}}\lesssim 1
. Then
(\eta,y,t)\mapsto F(\eta,y,t)[\varphi_{(\eta,y,t)}]
defines an element of
\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})
with

(3.86)

\|F(\eta,y,t)[\varphi_{(\eta,y,t)}]\|_{\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}}\lesssim\|F\|_{\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\Phi^{N^{\prime}}_{2\mathfrak{r}}}

as long as
\|F\|_{\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\Phi^{N^{\prime}}_{2\mathfrak{r}}}<\infty
and

N\geq N^{\prime}+4
. Furthermore

(3.87)

\|F(\eta,y,t)[\varphi_{(\eta,y,t)}]\|_{L^{p}_{\mu^{\mathfrak{p}}_{\Theta}}\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}}\lesssim\|F\|_{\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\Phi^{N^{\prime}}_{2\mathfrak{r}}}.

for any
p\in(0,+\infty]
and

(3.88)

\mu^{\mathfrak{p}}_{\Theta}\Bigl(\|F(\eta,y,t)[\varphi_{(\eta,y,t)}]\|_{\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}}>\lambda\Bigr)\leq\mu^{\mathfrak{p}}_{\Theta}\Bigl(\|F\|_{\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\Phi^{N^{\prime}}_{2\mathfrak{r}}}>C\lambda\Bigr).

Finally, for any
p\geq 1
it holds that that

(3.89)

\|F\|_{L^{p}_{\mu^{\mathfrak{p}}_{\Theta}}\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\Phi^{N}_{\mathfrak{r}}}\lesssim\sup_{\varphi}\|F(\eta,y,t)[\varphi]\|_{L^{p}_{\mu^{\mathfrak{p}}_{\Theta}}\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}},

where the upper bound is taken over all
\varphi\in\Phi^{\infty}_{2\mathfrak{r}}

with
\|\varphi\|_{\Phi^{N^{\prime}}_{2\mathfrak{r}}}\lesssim 1
, as long as
N\geq N^{\prime}+4
.

Proof.

Bounds (
3.86
) and

(
3.88
)
follow directly from

Lemma

3.13
noting that the quasi-triangle
inequality holds with constant
1
. From those two bounds one deduces
(
3.87
). For bound
(
3.89
) note that

(3.90)

\|F\|_{\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\Phi^{N}_{\mathfrak{r}}}\lesssim\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle k\rangle^{-(N-N^{\prime})}\|F\circ\Gamma[\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}]\|_{\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}}

with
\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}
given by
Lemma

3.13
. Thus

(3.91)

\mu^{\mathfrak{p}}_{\Theta}\left(\|F\|_{\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\Phi^{N}_{\mathfrak{r}}}>\lambda\right)\lesssim\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mu^{\mathfrak{p}}_{\Theta}\left(\|F[\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}]\|_{\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}}>\lambda C\langle k\rangle^{2}\right)

and

(3.92)

\|F\|_{L^{p}_{\mu^{\mathfrak{p}}_{\Theta}}\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\Phi^{N}_{\mathfrak{r}}}^{p}\begin{aligned} &\lesssim\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mu^{\mathfrak{p}}_{\Theta}\left(\|F[\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}]\|_{\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}}>\lambda^{\frac{1}{p}}C\langle k\rangle^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}\lambda\\
&\lesssim\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle k\rangle^{-2p}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mu^{\mathfrak{p}}_{\Theta}\left(\|F[\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}]\|_{\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}}>\lambda^{\frac{1}{p}}\right)\mathrm{d}\lambda\\
&=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle k\rangle^{-2p}\|F[\widetilde{\varphi_{k}}]\|_{\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}}^{p}.\end{aligned}

This shows (
3.89
).
∎

3.2.4.
Sizes: motivation

We conclude this section by providing some details and motivation for the
definition of the sizes introduced so far. As already discussed for the trees
themselves, the sizes are adapted to treating
\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}
as the group of
symmetries (
1.7
). If
\mathbb{S}_{\Theta}\Phi^{N}_{\mathfrak{r}}
is any of the
sizes
\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\Phi^{N}_{\mathfrak{r}}
,
\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\mathfrak{d}^{N}_{\mathfrak{r}}
,
\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\Box^{N}_{\mathfrak{r}}
, or
\mathbb{J}^{u}_{\Theta}\Phi^{N}_{\mathfrak{r}}
above, then setting

(3.93)

\widetilde{F}(\eta,y,t)=e^{2\pi i\xi(y-x)}F\Bigl(s(\eta-\xi),\frac{y-x}{s},\frac{t}{s}\Bigr)

it holds that

(3.94)

\|\widetilde{F}\|_{\mathbb{S}_{\Theta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T_{\Theta}(\xi^{\prime},x^{\prime},s^{\prime}))}=\|F\|_{\mathbb{S}_{\Theta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}\left(T_{\Theta}\left(s(\xi^{\prime}-\xi),\frac{x^{\prime}-x}{s},\frac{s^{\prime}}{s}\right)\right)}

and thus
\|\widetilde{F}\|_{\mathbb{S}_{\Theta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}}=\|F\|_{\mathbb{S}_{\Theta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}}
. Thanks to this fact and identities (
3.65
) and
(
3.61
), in our proofs we will often be able to restrict our
focus to
T=T_{\Theta}(0,0,1)
. Let us make this assumption also for the
discussion below that motivates the sizes we introduce and relates them to
well-known quantities in harmonic analysis.

The Lebesgue size
\mathbb{L}{}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}
of
Definition

3.8

controls the magnitude of the coefficients of the embedding on a tree. If
v=\infty
this is closely related to bounding a space and frequency localized
version of a Hardy-Littlewood maximal function. Let
\varphi\in\Phi^{N}_{\mathfrak{r}}
be
chosen to maximize
\mathrm{RHS}{\eqref{eq:lebesgue-size}}
for
F=\mathrm{Emb}[f]
.
Setting
\widetilde{\varphi}_{\theta}=\mathrm{Mod}_{\theta}(\varphi^{\vee})
we have

(3.95)

\bigl\|\mathrm{Emb}[f]\bigr\|_{\mathbb{L}{}^{(u,\infty)}_{\Theta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}\approx\int_{\Theta}\int_{B_{1}}\sup_{0<\sigma<1-|\zeta|}\left|f\ast\mathrm{Dil}_{\sigma}\widetilde{\varphi}_{\theta}(\zeta)\right|^{u}\frac{\mathrm{d}\zeta\mathrm{d}\theta}{\Theta}.

For every fixed
\theta\in\Theta
the inner integrand is a Hardy-Littlewood
maximal function over scales bounded from above. The lacunary size
\mathbb{L}^{(u,2)}_{\Theta}\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}
encodes the magnitude of a localized square
function. Let
\varphi\in\Phi^{N}_{\mathfrak{r}}
be chosen to extremize the upper bound
in the size
Definition

3.9
. Setting
\psi_{\theta}(z)=\mathrm{d}_{z}\left(\mathrm{Mod}_{\theta}(\varphi^{\vee})(z)\right)
we have that

(3.96)

\bigl\|\mathrm{Emb}[f]\bigr\|_{\mathbb{L}{}^{(u,2)}_{\Theta}\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}\approx\int_{\Theta}\int_{B_{1}}\left(\int_{0}^{1-|\zeta|}\left|f\ast\mathrm{Dil}_{\sigma}\psi_{\theta}(\zeta)\right|^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma}{\sigma}\right)^{u/2}\frac{\mathrm{d}\zeta\mathrm{d}\theta}{\Theta}.

For every fixed
\theta\in\Theta
the innermost integral is the
Littlewood-Paley square function. Note that
\psi_{\theta}
satisfies
\int\psi_{\theta}(z)\mathrm{d}z=0
.

Next, the
defect sizes
quantify by how much a function
F\in\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}){\otimes\Phi^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{r}}}^{\prime}
fails to be of the form
F=\mathrm{Emb}[f]
. The differential operators
t\mathrm{d}_{y}+2\pi i\xi_{T}
and
t\mathrm{d}_{t}-(\eta-\xi_{T})\mathrm{d}_{\eta}
appearing in
Definition

3.10
are the push
forwards of
\sigma\mathrm{d}_{\sigma}
and
\sigma\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}
using the map

\pi_{T}
. More explicitly

(3.97)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\sigma\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}(e^{2\pi i\xi_{T}s_{T}\zeta}F\circ\pi_{T}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma))\\
&\qquad=e^{2\pi i\xi_{T}s_{T}\zeta}(s_{T}\sigma\partial_{y}+2\pi i\xi_{T})F(\xi_{T}+\theta s_{T}^{-1}\sigma^{-1},x_{T}+s_{T}\zeta,s_{T}\sigma)\\
&\qquad=e^{2\pi i\xi_{T}s_{T}\zeta}\left(\left(t\mathrm{d}_{y}+2\pi i\xi_{T}\right)F(\eta,y,t)\right)\circ\pi_{T}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\sigma\mathrm{d}_{\sigma}(e^{2\pi i\xi_{T}s_{T}\zeta}F\circ\pi_{T}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma))\\
&\qquad=e^{2\pi i\xi_{T}s_{T}\zeta}(-\theta s_{T}^{-1}\sigma^{-1}\partial_{\eta}+s_{T}\sigma\partial_{t})F(\xi_{T}+\theta s_{T}^{-1}\sigma^{-1},x_{T}+s_{T}\zeta,s_{T}\sigma)\\
&\qquad=e^{2\pi i\xi_{T}s_{T}\zeta}\left(\left(t\mathrm{d}_{t}-(\eta-\xi_{T})\mathrm{d}_{\eta}\right)F(\eta,y,t)\right)\circ\pi_{T}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)\end{aligned}

A direct computation gives that for any
f\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R})
it
holds that

(3.98)

\displaystyle\sigma\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}\left(e^{2\pi i\xi_{T}s_{T}\zeta}\mathrm{Emb}[f]\circ\pi_{T}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)\right)=\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(\theta)\left(e^{2\pi i\xi_{T}s_{T}\zeta}\mathrm{Emb}[f]\circ\pi_{T}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)\right)

\displaystyle\sigma\mathrm{d}_{\sigma}\left(e^{2\pi i\xi_{T}s_{T}\zeta}\mathrm{Emb}[f]\circ\pi_{T}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)\right)=\mathfrak{d}_{\sigma}(\theta)\left(e^{2\pi i\xi_{T}s_{T}\zeta}\mathrm{Emb}[f]\circ\pi_{T}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)\right).

It follows that
\left\|\mathrm{Emb}[f]\right\|_{L^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\Box_{\zeta,\mathfrak{r}}^{N}}=\left\|\mathrm{Emb}[f]\right\|_{L^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\Box_{\sigma,\mathfrak{r}}^{N}}=0
. We actually use identity (
3.98
) to

define
the operators
\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(\theta)
and
\mathfrak{d}_{\sigma}(\theta)
acting on general functions
F\in\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}){\otimes\Phi^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{r}}}^{\prime}
not necessarily of the form
F=\mathrm{Emb}[f]
. In
(
3.74
) and (
3.76
) we postulate that the
operators
\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(\theta)
and
\mathfrak{d}_{\sigma}(\theta)
should act on
the argument
\Phi^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{r}}
of
F\circ\pi_{T_{\Theta}(0,0,1)}
in
the same way as the derivatives
\sigma\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}
and
\sigma\mathrm{d}_{\sigma}

act on
\mathrm{Emb}[f]\circ\pi_{T_{\Theta}(0,0,1)}
. The defect size then
encodes and quantifies the local failure of a function
F\in\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}){\otimes\Phi^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{r}}}^{\prime}
to be of the form
F=\mathrm{Emb}[f]
. We cannot
expect that the functions
F\in\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}){\otimes\Phi^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{r}}}^{\prime}

in play in the arguments of this paper to always be of the form
F=\mathrm{Emb}[f]
. Even in the process of defining size itself, described by
(
3.67
), we need to consider at least functions of the
form

(3.99)

F=\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}\setminus W^{-}}\mathrm{Emb}[f]

with W
{}^{-}\in\mathbb{T}^{\cup}_{\Theta}
. For the sake of discussion let us suppose
that
T=T_{\Theta}(0,0,1)
: the quantity intervening in the definition of

{\mathbb{L}_{\Theta}^{(u,v)}}\Box^{N}_{\zeta,\mathfrak{r}}(T)
then becomes

(3.100)

\left(\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(t\eta)-t\mathrm{d}_{y}\right)F(\eta,y,t)=-\left(t\mathrm{d}_{y}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}\setminus W^{-}}(\eta,y,t)\right)\mathrm{Emb}[f](\eta,y,t).

Based on the geometry of the set
W^{-}
, as explained in more detail further
down in
Lemma

3.20
, the term
\left(t\mathrm{d}_{y}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}\setminus W^{-}}(\eta,y,t)\right)
is a Radon measure supported on the
boundary of
W^{-}
. A similar discussion can be carried out for the size

{\mathbb{L}_{\Theta}^{(u,v)}}\Box^{N}_{\sigma,\mathfrak{r}}
. The crucial theme when
dealing with the defect is showing that mostly this is that generally we can
guarantee that the defect of the functions
F\in\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}){\otimes\Phi^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{r}}}^{\prime}
in play will resemble the one discussed above.

Finally, the
\mathbb{J}_{\Theta}^{u}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}
represents the maximal truncation
of a localized singular integral operator. To see this chose
\varphi\in\Phi^{N}_{\mathfrak{r}}
to extremize the upper bound in (
3.78
). Setting

\psi_{\theta}(z)=\mathrm{d}_{z}\left(\mathrm{Mod}_{\theta}(\varphi^{\vee})(z)\right)
and
computing the upper bound over
G
we get that

(3.101)

\|\mathrm{Emb}[f]\|_{\mathbb{J}_{\Theta}^{u}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}^{u}\lesssim\int_{\Theta}\int_{B_{1}}\sup_{\sigma<1-|\zeta|}\left|\int_{0}^{\sigma}f\ast\mathrm{Dil}_{\rho}\psi_{\theta}(\zeta)\frac{\mathrm{d}\rho}{\rho}\right|^{u}\frac{\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}\zeta}{|\Theta|}.

The inner integral is related and can be bound by the Hardy-Littlewood maximal
function of
f\ast K
where the kernel
K
is Calderón-Zygmund given by

(3.102)

K(z)\coloneqq\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{Dil}_{\rho}\psi_{\theta}(\zeta)\frac{\mathrm{d}\rho}{\rho}.

3.3.
Changes of variables and product sizes

The sizes that make up size
\mathbb{F}^{u}_{\Theta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}
, introduced in the
previous section, are well suited to control functions of the form
F=\mathrm{Emb}[f]\in\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}){\otimes\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}}^{\prime}
. To control
functions
\mathrm{Emb}[f]\circ\Gamma
appearing in
(
1.20
) we pull back the sizes via the
change of variables
\Gamma
. We consider changes of variables
\Gamma=\Gamma_{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)}
of the form

(3.103)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{(\alpha,\beta,\gamma)}(\eta,y,t)\coloneqq(\alpha(\eta+\gamma t^{-1}),y,\beta t),

\displaystyle\beta\in(0,1],\quad|\alpha\beta|\in\left(\frac{1}{2},2\right),\qquad|\gamma|<1\bm{}.

Let us introduce the notation
\theta_{\Gamma}\coloneqq\alpha\beta(\theta+\gamma)
, and let
\Theta_{\Gamma}\coloneqq\{\theta_{\Gamma}:\theta\in\Theta\}
. The former is an implicit function of
\theta\in\mathbb{R}
.

First we study how
\Gamma
acts on trees and forests. It holds that

\Gamma(T)\subset T_{\Theta_{\Gamma}}(\alpha\xi_{T},x_{T},s_{T})
and, in particular,
we have

(3.104)

\Gamma\circ\pi_{T}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)=\pi_{(\alpha\xi_{T},x_{T},s_{T})}(\theta_{\Gamma},\zeta,\beta\sigma);

furthermore,
\Gamma(W)\in\mathbb{T}^{\cup}_{\Theta_{\Gamma}}
for any
W\in\mathbb{T}^{\cup}_{\Theta}
. This implies that
\mu_{\Theta}^{\mathfrak{p}}(W)=\mu_{\Theta_{\Gamma}}^{\mathfrak{p}}(\Gamma(W))
. For any
f\in\mathscr{S}(\mathbb{R})
we have that (cfr. (
3.65
))

(3.105)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Emb}[f]\circ\Gamma\circ\pi_{T}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)

\displaystyle=e^{2\pi i\alpha\xi_{T}s_{T}\zeta}\mathrm{Emb}\left[s_{T}\mathrm{Dil}_{s_{T}^{-1}}\mathrm{Mod}_{-\alpha\xi_{T}}\mathrm{Tr}_{-x_{T}}f\right]\circ\pi_{(0,0,1)}(\theta_{\Gamma},\zeta,\beta\sigma).

Next, we define pullbacks of some of the sizes introduced above in

Section

3.2.2
: if
\mathbb{S}
is any of the local sizes we have so far defined we
ensure that for any
F\in\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}){\otimes\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}}^{\prime}
supported in compactly in
\Gamma(T)
we have that

(3.106)

{\|F\circ\Gamma\|_{\Gamma^{\ast}\mathbb{S}_{\Theta}(T)}}={\|F\|_{\mathbb{S}_{\Theta_{\Gamma}}(T_{{\Theta_{\Gamma}}}(\alpha\xi_{T},x_{T},s_{T}))}}.

The pullbacks of Lebesgue (local) sizes are then given by

(3.107)

\displaystyle\|F\|_{\Gamma^{\ast}\mathbb{L}{}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}(T)}\coloneqq\|F\|_{\mathbb{L}{}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}(T)};

the pullbacks of the lacunary (local) sizes are given by

(3.108)

\displaystyle\|F\|_{\Gamma^{\ast}{\mathbb{L}}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\mathfrak{d}_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}\coloneqq\left\|\Gamma^{\ast}\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(t(\eta-\xi_{T})){F}\right\|_{{\mathbb{L}}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\ast}\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(\theta)\coloneqq\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(\theta_{\Gamma});

the pullbacks of the defect (local) sizes are given by

(3.109)

\displaystyle\|F\|_{\Gamma^{\ast}{\mathbb{L}_{\Theta}^{(u,v)}}\Box_{\zeta,\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}:={\left\|\left(\Gamma^{\ast}\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(t(\eta-\xi_{T}))-\beta t\partial_{y}+2\pi i\alpha\xi_{T}\right)F\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{\Theta}^{(u,v)}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}},

\displaystyle\|F\|_{\Gamma^{\ast}\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\Box_{\sigma,\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}:=\left\|\left(\Gamma^{\ast}\mathfrak{d}_{\sigma}(t(\eta-\xi_{T}))-t\partial_{t}+(\eta-\xi_{T})\partial_{\eta}\right)F\right\|_{\mathbb{L}_{\Theta}^{(u,v)}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)},

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\ast}\mathfrak{d}_{\sigma}(\theta)\coloneqq\mathfrak{d}_{\sigma}(\theta_{\Gamma}).

and we set
\Gamma^{\ast}\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\Box_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)=\Gamma^{\ast}\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\Box_{\sigma,\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)+\Gamma^{\ast}\mathbb{L}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\Box_{\zeta,\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)
. Similarly
to the computation (
3.98
), using
(
3.105
), one can see that

(3.110)

\displaystyle\left(\Gamma^{\ast}\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(t(\eta-\xi_{T}))-\beta t\partial_{y}+2\pi i\alpha\xi_{T}\right)F

\displaystyle=\left(\Gamma^{\ast}\mathfrak{d}_{\sigma}(t(\eta-\xi_{T}))-t\partial_{t}+(\eta-\xi_{T})\partial_{\eta}\right)F=0

if
F=\mathrm{Emb}[f]\circ\Gamma
. We do not make use of the pullback of the
local sizes
\mathbb{J}_{\Theta_{\Gamma}}^{u}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)
since these sizes only
appear in relation to the term
\mathrm{Emb}[f_{1}]\circ\Gamma_{1}
, for which

\Gamma_{1}=\mathrm{Id}
.

We introduce frequency restricted variants of all the sizes above. Let us fix

\widetilde{\Theta}\subset\Theta
; denoting by
\mathbb{S}_{\Theta}
any of the (local)
sizes above we set

(3.111)

\|F\|_{\mathbb{S}_{(\Theta,\widetilde{\Theta})}(T)}:=\|\mathbbm{1}_{\pi_{T}\left(\mathfrak{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}\right)}F\|_{\mathbb{S}_{\Theta}(T)},

with
\mathfrak{T}{}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}\coloneqq\left\{(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)\in\widetilde{\Theta}\times B_{1}\times(0,1):\sigma<1-|\zeta|\right\}
.
Setting
\widetilde{\Theta}_{\Gamma}\coloneqq\{\theta_{\Gamma}:\theta\in\widetilde{\Theta}\}
, we have that

(3.112)

{\|F\circ\Gamma\|_{\Gamma^{\ast}\mathbb{S}_{(\Theta,\widetilde{\Theta})}(T)}}={\|F\|_{\mathbb{S}_{(\Theta_{\Gamma},\widetilde{\Theta}_{\Gamma})}(T_{{\Theta_{\Gamma}}}(\alpha\xi_{T},x_{T},s_{T}))}}

for any
F\in\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}){\otimes\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}}^{\prime}

supported compactly in
\Gamma(T)
. This concludes the definition of the
pullback sizes and provides us with all the elements necessary to understand
(
1.27
) that defines the size

\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}^{u}_{\Gamma}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}
. The single summands in
(
1.27
) are related to the local sizes
above through (
3.67
).

The sizes defined so far are well suited to control the single functions
F_{j}=\mathrm{Emb}[f_{j}]\circ\Gamma_{j}
. The size
\mathbb{I}_{\Theta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}
, defined
below, controls the product term

(3.113)

\prod_{j=1}^{3}\mathrm{Emb}[f_{j}]\circ\Gamma_{j}

appearing in
\mathrm{RHS}{\eqref{eq:BHT-dual-bounds}}
. We make sure that this size
is large enough to satisfy the hypothesis of
Proposition

3.5
to be able
to conclude that (
1.15
) holds. On the other hand, the
size
\mathbb{I}_{\Theta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}
has to be small enough to be able to control
it by the sizes
\mathbb{F}^{u}_{\Theta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}
of
\mathrm{Emb}[f_{1}]
by the sizes

\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}^{u_{j}}_{\Gamma_{j}}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}
and

\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}^{u_{\times}}_{\Gamma_{\times}}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}
, defined
subsequently, of the functions
\mathrm{Emb}[f_{j}]\circ\Gamma_{j}
,
j\in\{2,3\}

and of the function
\mathrm{Emb}[f_{2}]\circ\Gamma_{2}\mathrm{Emb}[f_{3}]\circ\Gamma_{3}
.
This is required to be able to show that
(
1.17
) and
(
1.21
) hold.

Definition 3.15

(Integral size)
.

Let us fix
\omega\in\Phi_{1}^{\infty}
such that

\widehat{\omega}\left(\widehat{z}\right)=1
for
\widehat{z}\in B_{7/8}
. For any

\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}/2}^{\infty}
and
j\in\{1,2,3\}
, we define

(3.114)

\displaystyle\varphi_{j,\theta}^{o}(z)\coloneqq\widehat{\varphi}(\theta_{\Gamma_{j}})\mathrm{Dil}_{\mathfrak{r}^{-1}}\omega(z),

\displaystyle\varphi_{j,\theta}^{l}(z)\coloneqq\varphi(z)-\widehat{\varphi}(\theta_{\Gamma_{j}})\mathrm{Dil}_{\mathfrak{r}^{-1}}\omega(z).

We define the local sizes
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{I}^{(\star_{1},\star_{2},\star_{3})}_{\Theta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}
with
(\star_{1},\star_{2},\star_{3})\in\{o,l\}^{3}
as follows. For
H\in\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})\otimes^{3}{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}}^{\prime}
and
T\in\mathbb{T}_{\Theta}
set

(3.115)

\displaystyle\|H\|_{\mathbb{I}^{(\star_{1},\star_{2},\star_{3})}_{\Theta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}\qquad

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sup_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{aligned} &\varphi\end{aligned}$}}}\liminf_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}\int_{\Theta}\left|\int_{\varepsilon}^{\infty}\left(\mathbbm{1}_{T}H\right)\circ\pi_{T}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)[\varphi_{1,\theta}^{\star_{1}},\varphi_{2,\theta}^{\star_{2}},\varphi_{3,\theta}^{\star_{3}}]\mathrm{d}\zeta\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma}{\sigma}\right|\frac{\mathrm{d}\theta}{|\Theta|},

where the upper bound is taken over
\varphi\in\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}/2}^{\infty}
with

\|\varphi\|_{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}/2}^{N}}\leq 1
. Using the local sizes above we have
given meaning to the definition (
1.16
) of the size

\mathbb{I}_{\Theta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}
. The single summands in (
1.16
) are
related to the local sizes above through (
3.67
).

We now define the (local) sizes used to control the product term
\mathrm{Emb}[f_{2}]\circ\Gamma_{2}\mathrm{Emb}[f_{3}]\circ\Gamma_{3}
appearing in
(
1.23
). Our procedure of bounding the term

(3.116)

\left\|\mathrm{Emb}[f_{1}]\mathrm{Emb}[f_{2}]\circ\Gamma_{2}\mathrm{Emb}[f_{3}]\circ\Gamma_{3}\right\|_{\mathbb{I}^{(l,o,o)}_{\Theta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}}

that appears in
Theorem

2
requires controlling quantities that cannot be
bounded uniformly by products of sizes of
\mathrm{Emb}[f_{2}]\circ\Gamma_{2}
and

\mathrm{Emb}[f_{3}]\circ\Gamma_{3}
. We believe that these additional quantities, that
arise in the proof of
Theorem

2
and that we encoded in the size

\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}^{u_{\times}}_{\Gamma_{\times}}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}
, is an
intrinsically bilinear feature dependent on the interaction between
\mathrm{Emb}[f_{2}]\circ\Gamma_{2}
and of
\mathrm{Emb}[f_{3}]\circ\Gamma_{3}
. For this reason, we
believe that our inability to control them using products of sizes of
\mathrm{Emb}[f_{2}]\circ\Gamma_{2}
and
\mathrm{Emb}[f_{3}]\circ\Gamma_{3}
is intrinsic and not a
shortcoming of the current method.

In general, given two function
G_{2},G_{3}\in L^{\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}){\otimes\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}}^{\prime}
their pointwise product is a
function in
G_{\times}\in L^{\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})\otimes^{2}{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}}^{\prime}
given by

(3.117)

G_{\times}(\eta,y,t)[\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3}]\coloneqq G_{2}(\eta,y,t)[\varphi_{2}]G_{3}(\eta,y,t)[\varphi_{3}].

This brings us to define the following product (local) sizes indexed by
T\in\mathbb{T}_{\Theta}
on functions
H\in\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})\otimes^{2}{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}}^{\prime}
. In our notation we “remember” both diffeomorphisms

\Gamma_{2}
and
\Gamma_{3}
by including the symbol
\Gamma_{\times}^{\ast}
.
The product Lebesgue local size is given by

(3.118)

\|H\|_{\Gamma_{\times}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}{}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}(\Phi\otimes\Phi)_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}\coloneqq\sup_{\text{\scriptsize{$\begin{aligned} &\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3}\end{aligned}$}}}\|H[\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3}]\|_{\mathbb{L}{}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}(T)}

where the upper bound is taken over all
\varphi_{j}\in\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}
with

\|\varphi_{j}\|_{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}}\leq 1
,
j\in\{2,3\}
. The product lacunary
local sizes, with
\theta_{\Gamma_{j}}\coloneqq\alpha_{j}\beta_{j}(\theta+\gamma_{j})
,
j\in\{2,3\}
, are given by

(3.119)

\displaystyle\|H\|_{\Gamma_{\times}^{\ast}{\mathbb{L}}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\left(\Phi\otimes\mathfrak{d}\right)_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}\coloneqq\left\|\left(\mathrm{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{\ast}_{3}\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(t(\eta-\xi_{T}))\right)H\right\|_{\Gamma_{\times}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}{}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}(\Phi\otimes\Phi)_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)},

\displaystyle\|H\|_{\Gamma_{\times}^{\ast}{\mathbb{L}}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\left(\mathfrak{d}\otimes\Phi\right)_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}\coloneqq\left\|\left(\Gamma^{\ast}_{2}\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(t(\eta-\xi_{T}))\otimes\mathrm{Id}\right)H\right\|_{\Gamma_{\times}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}{}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}(\Phi\otimes\Phi)_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)},

\displaystyle\|H\|_{\Gamma_{\times}^{\#}{\mathbb{L}}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}\left(\mathfrak{d}\otimes\mathfrak{d}\right)_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\|\left(\Gamma^{\ast}_{2}\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(t(\eta-\xi_{T}))\otimes\Gamma^{\ast}_{3}\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(t(\eta-\xi_{T}))\right){H}\right\|_{\Gamma_{\times}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}{}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}(\Phi\otimes\Phi)_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}.

The linear operators
\left(\mathrm{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{\ast}_{3}\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(\theta)\right)
,
\left(\Gamma^{\ast}_{2}\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(\theta)\otimes\mathrm{Id}\right)
, and
\left(\Gamma^{\ast}_{2}\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(\theta)\otimes\Gamma^{\ast}_{3}\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(\theta)\right)
, defined on functions
H\in\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})\otimes^{2}{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}}^{\prime}
, are given by

(3.120)

\displaystyle\left(\mathrm{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{\ast}_{3}\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(\theta)\right)H(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)[\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3}]\coloneqq H(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)[\varphi_{2},(-d_{z}+2\pi i\theta_{\Gamma_{3}})\varphi_{3}(z)],

\displaystyle\left(\Gamma^{\ast}_{2}\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(\theta)\otimes\mathrm{Id}\right)H(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)[\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3}]\coloneqq H(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)[(-d_{z}+2\pi i\theta_{\Gamma_{2}})\varphi_{2}(z),\varphi_{3}],

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\left(\Gamma^{\ast}_{2}\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(\theta)\otimes\Gamma^{\ast}_{3}\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(\theta)\right)H(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)[\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3}]\qquad\\
&\qquad\coloneqq H(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)[(-d_{z}+2\pi i\theta_{\Gamma_{2}})\varphi_{2}(z),(-d_{z}+2\pi i\theta_{\Gamma_{3}})\varphi_{2}(z)].\end{aligned}

To define product defect sizes, we also introduce the operators
\left(\mathrm{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{\ast}_{3}\mathfrak{d}_{\sigma}(\theta)\right)
,
\left(\Gamma^{\ast}_{2}\mathfrak{d}_{\sigma}(\theta)\otimes\mathrm{Id}\right)
and
\left(\Gamma^{\ast}_{2}\mathfrak{d}_{\sigma}(\theta)\otimes\Gamma^{\ast}_{3}\mathfrak{d}_{\sigma}(\theta)\right)
analogously to (
3.120
) with
\mathfrak{d}_{\sigma}

in place of
\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}
. We set

(3.121)

\displaystyle\|H\|_{\Gamma^{\ast}_{\times}{\mathbb{L}_{\Theta}^{(u,v)}}\Box_{\zeta,\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\|\left(\Gamma^{\ast}_{2}\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(\theta)\otimes\operatorname{Id}+\operatorname{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{\ast}_{3}\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(\theta)-\beta t\mathrm{d}_{y}+2\pi i(\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3})\xi_{T}\right)H\right\|_{\Gamma_{\times}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}{}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}(\Phi\otimes\Phi)_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)},

(3.122)

\displaystyle\|H\|_{\Gamma^{\ast}_{\times}{\mathbb{L}_{\Theta}^{(u,v)}}\Box_{\sigma,\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\begin{aligned} &\left\|\left(\Gamma^{\ast}_{2}\mathfrak{d}_{\sigma}(\theta)\otimes\operatorname{Id}+\operatorname{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{\ast}_{3}\mathfrak{d}_{\sigma}(\theta)-t\mathrm{d}_{t}+(\eta-\xi_{T})\mathrm{d}_{\eta}\right)H\right\|_{\Gamma_{\times}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}{}^{(u,v)}_{\Theta}(\Phi\otimes\Phi)_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}.\end{aligned}

Similarly to the computation (
3.98
), using
(
3.105
), one can see that

(3.123)

\displaystyle\left(\Gamma^{\ast}_{2}\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(\theta)\otimes\operatorname{Id}+\operatorname{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{\ast}_{3}\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(\theta)-\beta t\mathrm{d}_{y}+2\pi i(\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3})\xi_{T}\right)H

\displaystyle=\left(\Gamma^{\ast}_{2}\mathfrak{d}_{\sigma}(\theta)\otimes\operatorname{Id}+\operatorname{Id}\otimes\Gamma^{\ast}_{3}\mathfrak{d}_{\sigma}(\theta)-t\mathrm{d}_{t}+(\eta-\xi_{T})\mathrm{d}_{\eta}\right)H=0

if
H=\mathrm{Emb}[f_{2}]\circ\Gamma_{2}\mathrm{Emb}[f_{3}]\circ\Gamma_{3}
. We denote

\Gamma^{\ast}_{\times}{\mathbb{L}_{\Theta}^{(u,v)}}\Box_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)\coloneqq\Gamma^{\ast}_{\times}{\mathbb{L}_{\Theta}^{(u,v)}}\Box_{\zeta,\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)+\Gamma^{\ast}_{\times}{\mathbb{L}_{\Theta}^{(u,v)}}\Box_{\sigma,\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)
. This concludes the definition of the bilinear sizes and provides us with
all the elements necessary to understand
(
1.28
) that defines the size

\mathbb{F}^{u}_{\Theta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}
. The single summands in
(
1.28
) are related to the local sizes
above through (
3.67
).

(3.124)

3.4.
Localized outer Lebesgue
spaces

In this section we localize the outer Lebesgue quasi-norm

L^{p}_{\mu^{\mathfrak{p}}_{\Theta}}\mathbb{S}
to spatial regions. Iterated outer measure
spaces appear already in the context of non-uniform bounds in
(
1.10
). In the range of exponents
p_{j}\in(2,\infty]
,

j\in\{1,2,3\}
(triangle
c
in
Figure

1
) it is possible to
reason at the level of non-iterated outer Lebesgue quasi-norms both for
non-uniform (see
[
DT15
]
) and uniform bounds (we do
not elaborate on this in this paper). Introducing localization is necessary to
prove (
1.10
) outside of the regime
p_{j}\in(2,\infty]
.
This necessity is dictated by the fact that the embedding bounds

(3.125)

\left\|\mathrm{Emb}[f_{j}]\circ\Gamma_{j}\right\|_{L^{p_{j}}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta}}\mathbb{S}_{\operatorname{en},j}}\lesssim\|f_{j}\|_{L^{p_{j}}(\mathbb{R})}.

are known not to hold when
p_{j}\leq 2
. Iterated outer Lebesgue norms can be
thought of as appropriate generalizations of product Lebesgue norms to the
outer Lebesgue setting. The symbols
\not{L}^{q,+}_{\mu_{\Theta}^{\infty},\nu_{1}}
,
\not{L}^{q,+}_{\mu_{\Theta}^{1},\nu_{\beta}}
, and
X^{q,r,+}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}
, defined subsequently, play the role of
sizes on analogues of fibers (in our setup: strips). These sizes are
themselves outer Lebesgue quasi-norms on said fibers. This is completely
analogous to the classical fact that the product Lebesgue norm

(3.126)

\|f(x,y)\|_{L^{p}_{x\in\mathbb{R}}L^{q}_{y\in\mathbb{R}}}

is defined by taking the Lebesgue norm
L^{p}_{x\in\mathbb{R}}
of the function
x\mapsto\|f(x,y)\|_{L^{q}_{y\in\mathbb{R}}}
where, effectively, the magnitude of
the function on each fiber
\{x\}\times\mathbb{R}
is measured by taking its
L^{q}

norm. The difficulty in our setup is that different fibers, or “strips”, are
not necessarily disjoint in largely the same way as different trees are not
necessarily disjoint either.

We begin by introducing a family of distinguished sets:

\beta
-
strips
. Countable unions thereof will play the role of a

\sigma
-generating collection As for trees (
Definition

3.6
) and sizes in

Section

3.2.2
, our localization procedure must be adapted to each map

\Gamma_{j}
separately. This calls for having strips depend on the parameter

\beta\in(0,1]
.

Definition 3.16

(Strips and strip outer measure)
.

Let
\beta\in(0,1]
. A strip
D_{\beta}(x_{D},s_{D})
with top
(x_{D},s_{D})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}
is given by

(3.127)

D_{\beta}(x_{D},s_{D})=\{(\eta,y,t):\beta t<\beta(s_{T}-|y-x_{T}|)\}.

We denote by
\mathbb{D}_{\beta}
the collection of all strips
D_{\beta}(x_{T},s_{T})
. The outer measure
\nu_{\beta}(E)
is given by

(3.128)

\displaystyle\nu_{\beta}(E)\coloneqq\inf\left\{\|N_{\mathcal{D}}(z)\|_{L^{1}_{\mathrm{d}z}(\mathbb{R})}:\mathcal{D}\subset\mathbb{D}_{\beta},\hskip 2.70004ptE\subset\bigcup_{D\in\mathcal{D}}D\right\},

\displaystyle N_{\mathcal{D}}(z)\coloneqq\sum_{D\in\mathcal{D}}\mathbbm{1}_{B_{s_{T}}(x_{T})}(z).

There is a one-to-one correspondence between the datum
\beta,(x_{D},s_{D})
and
a given strip. We call
B_{s_{D}}(x_{D})
the spatial interval of a strip. We
denote countable unions of strips in
\mathbb{D}_{\beta}
by
\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\cup}
. We define two ways to localize the outer Lebesgue quasi-norms

L^{q}_{\mu^{\mathfrak{p}}_{\Theta}}\mathbb{S}
to unions of strips; here
\mathbb{S}
can be any
size on
\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})\otimes^{k}{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}}^{\prime}
.

Definition 3.17

(Localized outer Lebesgue quasi-norms)
.

Let
\beta\in(0,1]
and let
\mathbb{S}
be
a size on
\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})\otimes^{k}{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}}^{\prime}
; we
define the localized outer Lebesgue (local) sizes

\not{L}^{q}_{\mu^{\infty}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}
and
\not{L}^{q,+}_{\mu^{\infty}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}
as follows. For
\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})\otimes^{k}{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}}^{\prime}
and
V^{+}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\cup}
set

(3.129)

\displaystyle\|F\|_{\not{L}^{q}_{\mu^{\infty}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}(V^{+})}\coloneqq\left\|\mathbbm{1}_{V^{+}}F\right\|_{L^{q}_{\mu^{\infty}_{\Theta}}\mathbb{S}}

\displaystyle\|F\|_{\not{L}^{q,+}_{\mu^{\infty}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}(V^{+})}\coloneqq\sup_{\overline{q}\in[q,\infty]}\|F\|_{\not{L}^{\overline{q}}_{\mu^{\infty}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}(V^{+})}.

Similarly, we define
\not{L}^{q}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}
and
\not{L}^{q,+}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}
; for
F\in\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})\otimes^{k}{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}}^{\prime}
and
V^{+}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\cup}
set

(3.130)

\displaystyle\|F\|_{\not{L}^{q}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}(V^{+})}\coloneqq\nu_{\beta}(V^{+})^{-\frac{1}{q}}\left\|\mathbbm{1}_{V^{+}}F\right\|_{L^{q}_{\mu_{\Theta}^{1}}\mathbb{S}(V^{+})},

\displaystyle\|F\|_{\not{L}^{q,+}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}(V^{+})}\coloneqq\sup_{\overline{q}\in[q,\infty]}\|F\|_{\not{L}^{\overline{q}}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}(V^{+})}.

We then set, analogously to (
3.67
),

(3.131)

\displaystyle\|F\|_{\not{L}^{q}_{\mu^{\infty}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}}\coloneqq\sup_{V^{+}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\cup}}\sup_{V^{-}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\cup}}\left\|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}\setminus V^{-}}F\right\|_{\not{L}^{q}_{\mu^{\infty}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}(V^{+})},

\displaystyle\|F\|_{\not{L}^{q,+}_{\mu^{\infty}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}}\coloneqq\sup_{V^{+}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\cup}}\sup_{V^{-}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\cup}}\left\|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}\setminus V^{-}}F\right\|_{\not{L}^{q,+}_{\mu^{\infty}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}(V^{+})},

\displaystyle\|F\|_{\not{L}^{q}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}}\coloneqq\sup_{V^{+}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\cup}}\sup_{V^{-}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\cup}}\left\|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}\setminus V^{-}}F\right\|_{\not{L}^{q}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}(V^{+})},

\displaystyle\|F\|_{\not{L}^{q,+}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}}\coloneqq\sup_{V^{+}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\cup}}\sup_{V^{-}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\cup}}\left\|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}\setminus V^{-}}F\right\|_{\not{L}^{q,+}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}(V^{+})},

to obtain the corresponding sizes.

We also define the local sizes
X^{q,r,+}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}
as follows. For
F\in\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})\otimes^{k}{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}}^{\prime}
and
V^{+}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\cup}
set

(3.132)

\displaystyle\|F\|_{X^{q,r}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}(V^{+})}\coloneqq\nu_{\beta}(V^{+})^{-\frac{1}{r}}\sup_{W^{+}\in\mathbb{T}^{\cup}_{\Theta}}\left(\mu^{\infty}_{\Theta}(W^{+})^{\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{r}}\left\|\mathbbm{1}_{(V^{+}\cap W^{+})}F\right\|_{L^{r}_{\mu_{\Theta}^{1}}\mathbb{S}}\right),

\displaystyle\|F\|_{X^{q,r,+}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}(V^{+})}\coloneqq\sup_{\overline{r}\in[r,q]}\|F\|_{X^{q,\overline{r}}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}(V^{+})}

and we get the corresponding sizes by setting

(3.133)

\displaystyle\|F\|_{X^{q,r}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}}\coloneqq\sup_{V^{+}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\cup}}\sup_{V^{-}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\cup}}\left\|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}\setminus V^{-}}F\right\|_{X^{q,r}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}(V^{+})},

\displaystyle\|F\|_{X^{q,r,+}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}}\coloneqq\sup_{V^{+}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\cup}}\sup_{V^{-}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\cup}}\left\|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}\setminus V^{-}}F\right\|_{X^{q,r,+}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}(V^{+})}.

Let us now discuss the meaning of the sizes above. The sizes
\|F\|_{\not{L}^{q}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}}
were first introduced in

[
Ura16
]
(cfr.

[
Ura16
, (2.14)]
) albeit their local
variants were indexed by strips
D\in\mathbb{D}_{1}
rather than by countable unions

V_{+}
thereof, as in
Definition

3.17
. For the case
\beta=1
these two approaches are functionally the same while for
\beta\ll 1

they yield non uniformly equivalent sizes. Note that the sizes
\not{L}^{q,+}_{\mu^{\mathfrak{p}}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}
are monotone in
q
in the sense
that
\|F\|_{\not{L}^{q,+}_{\mu^{\mathfrak{p}}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}}\geq\|F\|_{\not{L}^{\overline{q},+}_{\mu^{\mathfrak{p}}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}}
if
q<\overline{q}
.

The size
X^{q,r}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}
are weaker variants of
the sizes
\|F\|_{\not{L}^{q}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}}
. Clearly

(3.134)

\|F\|_{\left(X^{q,q}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}\right)(V)}=\nu_{\beta}(V)^{-\frac{1}{q}}\left\|\mathbbm{1}_{V}F\right\|_{L^{q}_{\mu_{\Theta}^{1}}\mathbb{S}}=\left\|\mathbbm{1}_{V}F\right\|_{\not{L}^{q}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}(V^{+})}.

On the other hand, for
r\leq q
we have that

(3.135)

\displaystyle\|F\|_{\left(X^{q,r}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}\right)(V)}=\sup_{W\in\mathbb{T}^{\cup}_{\Theta}}\nu_{\beta}(V)^{-\frac{1}{r}}\mu^{\infty}_{\Theta}(W)^{\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{r}}\left\|\mathbbm{1}_{V}\mathbbm{1}_{W}F\right\|_{L^{r}_{\mu_{\Theta}^{1}}\mathbb{S}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{W\in\mathbb{T}^{\cup}_{\Theta}}\beta^{-\frac{1}{r}}\mu^{\infty}_{\Theta}(W)^{\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{r}}\left\|\mathbbm{1}_{V}\mathbbm{1}_{W}F\right\|_{L^{r}_{\mu_{\Theta}^{\infty}}\mathbb{S}}\leq\beta^{-\frac{1}{r}}\left\|\mathbbm{1}_{V}F\right\|_{L^{q}_{\mu_{\Theta}^{\infty}}\mathbb{S}}

showing that
\|F\|_{\left(X^{q,1,+}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}\right)(V)}\leq\beta^{-1}\|F\|_{\not{L}^{q}_{\mu^{\infty}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}(V)}
. In practice, this means that the local sizes
\|F\|_{\left(X^{q,r,+}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}\right)(V)}
are
weaker than
\|F\|_{\not{L}^{q}_{\mu^{\infty}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}(V)}
for

\beta=1
but they may be better behaved when
\beta
is small.

The claimed bound above follows from the following lemma.

Lemma 3.18
.

Given any
V\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\cup}

and any
W\in\mathbb{T}^{\cup}_{\Theta}
it holds that

(3.136)

\mu^{1}_{\Theta}(W\cap V)\leq 4\beta^{-1}\nu_{\beta}(V)\mu^{\infty}_{\Theta}(W\cap V).

Proof.

Given any strip
D\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta}
and any tree
T\in\mathbb{T}_{\Theta}
it holds that

(3.137)

T(\xi_{T},x_{D,T},2\beta^{-1}s_{D,T})\supset T\cap D

where
B_{s_{D,T}}(x_{D,T})\coloneqq I_{T}\cap I_{D}
. From this it follows that

(3.138)

\mu^{1}_{\Theta}(W\cap V)\leq 2\beta^{-1}\nu(V)\mu^{\infty}_{\Theta}(W\cap V)

for any
V\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\cup}
and any
W\in\mathbb{T}^{\cup}_{\Theta}
. As a
matter of fact, let
\mathcal{D}\subset\mathbb{D}_{\beta}
be such that
V\subset\bigcup_{D\in\mathcal{D}}D
and
\|N_{\mathcal{D}}\|_{L^{1}}\leq 2\nu_{\beta}(V)
and let
\mathcal{T}\subset\mathbb{T}_{\Theta}
be such that

(3.139)

\left\|N_{\mathcal{T}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq 2\mu_{\Theta}^{\infty}(W\cap V),\qquad W\cap V\subset\bigcup_{T\in\mathcal{T}}T.

It then holds that

(3.140)

\sum_{D\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}}|I_{D}\cap I_{T}|=\int\sum_{D\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}}\mathbbm{1}_{I_{D}}(z)\mathbbm{1}_{I_{T}}(z)\mathrm{d}z\leq\left\|N_{\mathcal{D}}\right\|_{L^{1}}\left\|N_{\mathcal{T}}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Since
W\cap V\subset\bigcup_{T\in\mathcal{T}}\bigcup_{D\in\mathcal{D}}(D\cap T)
we have that

(3.141)

W\cap V\subset\bigcup_{D\in\mathcal{D}}\bigcup_{T\in\mathcal{T}}T(\xi_{T},x_{D,T},2\beta^{-1}s_{D,T}),

so
\mu^{1}_{\Theta}(W\cap V)\leq\sum_{D\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}}4\beta^{-1}s_{D,T}=\sum_{D\in\mathcal{D}}\sum_{T\in\mathcal{T}}2\beta^{-1}|I_{D}\cap I_{T}|
, as required
∎

Finally, note that the sizes
X^{q,r,+}_{\mu^{\mathfrak{p}}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}
are monotone in
q
and
r
in the sense that
\|F\|_{X^{q,r,+}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}}\geq\|F\|_{\not{L}^{\overline{q},r,+}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}}
if
q<\overline{q}
and
\|F\|_{X^{q,r,+}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}}\geq\|F\|_{\not{L}^{q,\overline{r},+}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}}
if
r<\overline{r}
.

When proving iterated bounds of the form
(
1.19
) and
(
1.20
) it is necessary to unwrap the
sequence of definitions above; since this is going to be a recurrent procedure
in this work we find it useful to explicitly go through this procedure in the
following remark.

Remark 3.19
.

Let us unwrap

Definition

3.17
: in that setup suppose we need to show
that

(3.142)

\|F\|_{L^{p}_{\nu_{\beta}}\not{L}^{q}_{\mu^{\mathfrak{p}}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}}\lesssim A\text{}

with some
A\in[0,+\infty)
and an implicit constant independent of
A

and
F\in\mathscr{R}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})\otimes^{k}{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}}^{\prime}
. This is
tantamount to exhibiting a choice of sets
{V^{-}}(\lambda)\in\mathbb{D}^{\cup}_{\beta}
for any
\lambda\in(0,+\infty)
such that

(3.143)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\nu_{\beta}\left({V^{-}}(\lambda^{1/p})\right)\mathrm{d}\lambda\lesssim A^{p},\qquad\left\|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}{\setminus V^{-}}(\lambda)}F\right\|_{\not{L}^{q}_{\mu^{\mathfrak{p}}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}}\lesssim\lambda.

The second condition amounts to exhibiting a choice of sets
W_{q}(\tau,V^{+},V^{-})\in\mathbb{T}_{\Theta}^{\cup}
for any
V^{\pm}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\cup}
and

\tau\in(0,+\infty)
such that if
V^{-}\supseteq\overline{V}(\lambda)
then

(3.144)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\mu_{\Theta}^{\mathfrak{p}}(W_{q}(\tau^{1/q},V^{+},V^{-}))\mathrm{d}\tau\lesssim\lambda^{q}\nu_{\beta}(V^{+})^{\frac{1}{\mathfrak{p}}},

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbbm{1}_{V^{+}\setminus(V^{-}\cup W_{q}(\lambda,\tau,V^{+},V^{-}))}F\right\|_{\mathbb{S}}\lesssim\tau.

When we are showing
\|F\|_{L^{p}_{\nu_{\beta}}\not{L}^{q,+}_{\mu^{\mathfrak{p}}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}}\lesssim A
, then we need to
show that (
3.143
) holds with
\overline{q}
in
place of
q
, for all
\overline{q}\in\{q,+\infty\}
. This follows by log
convexity of outer Lebesgue quasi-norms ((
3.19
) in

Proposition

3.1
). All the implicit constants above cannot
depend on any of the variable quantities
\lambda,\tau,V^{\pm}
.

Similarly, showing that

(3.145)

\|F\|_{L^{p}_{\nu_{\beta}}X^{q,r,+}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}}\lesssim A

amounts to exhibiting a choice of sets
{V^{-}}(\lambda)\in\mathbb{D}^{\cup}_{\beta}
for any
\lambda\in(0,+\infty)
such that

(3.146)

\int_{0}^{\infty}\nu_{\beta}\left({V^{-}}(\lambda^{1/p})\right)\mathrm{d}\lambda\lesssim A^{p},\qquad\left\|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}{\setminus V^{-}}(\lambda)}F\right\|_{X^{q,r,+}_{\mu^{1}_{\Theta},\nu_{\beta}}\mathbb{S}}\lesssim\lambda.

According to (
3.133
) and using log convexity of outer Lebesgue
quasi-norms ((
3.19
) in

Proposition

3.1
). The second bound amount to showing that for
any
V^{+}\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\cup}
,
W^{+}\in\mathbb{T}^{\cup}_{\Theta}
, it holds
that

(3.147)

\left\|\mathbbm{1}_{(V^{+}\cap W^{+})\setminus V^{-}(\lambda)}F\right\|_{L^{\overline{r}}_{\mu_{\Theta}^{1}}\mathbb{S}}\lesssim\lambda\nu_{\beta}(V^{+})^{\frac{1}{\overline{r}}}\mu^{\infty}_{\Theta}(W^{+})^{\frac{1}{\overline{r}}-\frac{1}{q}}

with
\overline{r}\in\{r,q\}
.

We conclude this section with a lemma that encodes how the boundary of sets
V\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\cup}
and
W\in\mathbb{T}_{\Theta}^{\cup}
looks like. We showed
in
Section

3.2.4
that this is of crucial importance because the
defect size of a function of the form

(3.148)

\mathbbm{1}_{(V^{+}\cap W^{+})\setminus(V^{-}\cup W^{-})}\mathrm{Emb}[f]\circ\Gamma

is going to be a bound on a singular measure supported on the boundary of

(V^{+}\cap W^{+})\setminus(V^{-}\cup W^{-})
.

Lemma 3.20

(Geometry of the boundary of generating sets)
.

Let

V\in\mathbb{D}_{\beta}^{\cup}
,
W_{1},\ldots,W_{n}\in\mathbb{T}_{\Theta}^{\cup}
and
E
a set of the form
E=V\cap\left(\bigcap_{l=1}^{k}W\right)
or
E=V\cup\left(\bigcup_{l=1}^{k}W\right)
. The boundary of
E
is described by the graph of a function

\mathfrak{b}_{E}:\mathbb{R}^{2}\rightarrow[0,\infty]
i.e.

(3.149)

{E}=\left\{(\eta,y,t)\in\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}:t\in\left(0,\mathfrak{b}_{E}(\eta,y)\right)\right\}.

The function
\mathfrak{b}_{E}
is locally Lipschitz; if we denote
\Theta=(\theta_{-},\theta_{+})
then it holds that

(3.150)

\left|\partial_{y}\mathfrak{b}_{E}(\eta,y)\right|\leq\beta^{-1},\qquad\frac{\mathfrak{b}_{E}(\eta,y)}{-\theta_{+}}\leq\frac{\partial_{\eta}\mathfrak{b}_{E}(\eta,y)}{\mathfrak{b}_{E}(\eta,y)}\leq\frac{\mathfrak{b}_{E}(\eta,y)}{-\theta_{-}}.

The distributional derivative
t\mathrm{d}_{t}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(\eta,y,t)
is given by
measure
t\delta\left(t-\mathfrak{b}_{E}(\eta,y)\right)
. Given any
F\in L^{\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})
and any
\mathfrak{b}:\mathbb{R}^{2}\rightarrow[0,\infty]
satisfying (
3.150
) it holds
that

(3.151)

\displaystyle\left\|F(\eta,y,t)t\delta\left(t-\mathfrak{b}(\eta,y)\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{(u,1)}_{(\Theta,\widetilde{\Theta})}(T)}

\displaystyle\approx\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{\pi_{T}\left(\mathfrak{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}\right)}\left(\eta,y,\mathfrak{b}(\eta,y)\right)\left|F\left(\eta,y,\mathfrak{b}(\eta,y)\right)\right|^{u}\mathfrak{b}(\eta,y)\frac{\mathrm{d}\eta\mathrm{d}y}{|\Theta|s_{T}}\right)^{\frac{1}{u}}.

The statement above has an analogue in local coordinates given by
\pi_{T}
.
Let
T\in\mathbb{T}_{\Theta}
and let
\mathfrak{b}:\mathbb{R}^{2}\rightarrow[0,\infty]

be a locally Lipschitz function that satisfies

(
3.150
)
. Then there exists a function

\mathfrak{b}^{\ast}:\Theta\times B_{1}\rightarrow[0,+\infty)
such that

(3.152)

\pi_{T}\left(\left\{(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)\in\mathfrak{T}_{\Theta}:\sigma\leq\mathfrak{b}^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta)\right\}\right)=\left\{(\eta,y,t)\in T:t<\mathfrak{b}(\eta,y)\right\}.

This function
\mathfrak{b}^{\ast}
satisfies

(3.153)

\left|\partial_{\zeta}\mathfrak{b}^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta)\right|\leq\beta^{-1},\qquad\frac{1}{\theta-\theta_{+}}\leq\frac{\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{b}^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta)}{\mathfrak{b}^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta)}\leq\frac{1}{\theta-\theta_{-}}.

Given any
F\in L^{\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+})
it holds
that

(3.154)

\displaystyle\left\|F(\eta,y,t)t\delta\left(t-\mathfrak{b}(\eta,y)\right)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{(u,1)}_{(\Theta,\widetilde{\Theta})}(T)}

\displaystyle\approx\left(\int_{\widetilde{\Theta}\times B_{1}}\left|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}}(F\circ\pi_{T})\left(\theta,\zeta,\mathfrak{b}^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta)\right)\right|^{u}\frac{\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}\zeta}{|\Theta|}\right)^{\frac{1}{u}}.

All the implicit constants are uniformly bounded from above and away from

0
as long as
\mathrm{dist}\left(\widetilde{\Theta},\mathbb{R}\setminus\Theta\right)
is
bounded away from
0
.

Proof.

The bound (
3.150
) holds for
E=D_{\beta}(x,s)

and
E=T_{\Theta}(\xi,x,s)
since, explicitly,

(3.155)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}_{D_{\beta}(x,s)}(\eta,y)=\max(\beta^{-1}(s-|y-x|),0),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}_{T_{\Theta}(\xi,x,s)}(\eta,y)=\min\left(\max(\beta^{-1}(s-|y-x|),0),\mathbbm{1}_{\eta>0}\frac{\theta_{+}}{|\eta-\xi|}+\mathbbm{1}_{\eta<0}\frac{\theta_{-}}{|\eta-\xi|}\right),

where
\Theta=(\theta_{-},\theta_{+})
. The bounds
(
3.150
) follow since for any collection
E_{i}
it
holds that

(3.156)

\mathfrak{b}_{\bigcup_{i}E_{i}}(\eta,y)=\sup_{i}\mathfrak{b}_{E_{i}}(\eta,y)\qquad\mathfrak{b}_{\bigcap_{i}E_{i}}(\eta,y)=\inf_{i}\mathfrak{b}_{E_{i}}(\eta,y)

and the conditions (
3.150
) are stable with respect to
the operations above.

Now, given
\mathfrak{b}_{E}:\mathbb{R}^{2}\rightarrow[0,\infty]
let us construct

\mathfrak{b}^{\ast}:\Theta\times B_{1}\rightarrow[0,+\infty)
. Let us define

(3.157)

\mathfrak{b}^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta)\coloneqq\sup\left\{\sigma:s_{T}\sigma\leq\mathfrak{b}(\xi_{T}+\theta(s_{T}\sigma)^{-1},x_{T}+s_{T}\zeta)\right\}

that gives
\mathfrak{b}^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta)\geq 0
since
\mathfrak{b}(\eta,y)\geq 0
. Note that the function

(3.158)

\sigma\mapsto s_{T}\sigma-\mathfrak{b}_{E}(\xi_{T}+\theta(s_{T}\sigma)^{-1},x_{T}+s_{T}\zeta)

is continuous and for any
\theta\in\Theta
and
\zeta\in\mathbb{R}
it holds
that

(3.159)

\frac{\mathrm{d}_{\sigma}}{s_{T}}\left(s_{T}\sigma-\mathfrak{b}_{E}(\xi_{T}+\theta(s_{T}\sigma)^{-1},x_{T}+s_{T}\zeta)\right)=1+\theta\frac{\partial_{\eta}\mathfrak{b}_{E}(\xi_{T}+\theta(s_{T}\sigma)^{-1},x_{T}+s_{T}\zeta)}{s_{T}\sigma^{2}}.

Thus if
s_{T}\sigma-\mathfrak{b}_{E}(\xi_{T}+\theta(s_{T}\sigma)^{-1},x_{T}+s_{T}\zeta)
is in a small neighborhood of
0
and
\theta\in\Theta=(\theta_{-},\theta_{+})
it holds, thank to (
3.150
),
that

(3.160)

\frac{\mathrm{d}_{\sigma}}{s_{T}}\left(s_{T}\sigma-\mathfrak{b}(\xi_{T}+\theta(s_{T}\sigma)^{-1},x_{T}+s_{T}\zeta)\right)>0.

It follows that
s_{T}\sigma-\mathfrak{b}_{E}(\xi_{T}+\theta(s_{T}\sigma)^{-1},x_{T}+s_{T}\zeta)
can change sign only once and from
<0
to
>0
. This
shows (
3.152
). We can now differentiate

(3.161)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}(\eta,y)=s_{T}\mathfrak{b}^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}(\eta,y)(\eta-\xi_{T})=\theta,

\displaystyle y=x_{T}+s_{T}\zeta

to obtain

(3.162)

\left|\partial_{\zeta}\mathfrak{b}^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta)\right|\leq\beta^{-1}

and

(3.163)

\partial_{\eta}\mathfrak{b}(\eta,y)=s_{T}\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{b}^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta)\left(\partial_{\eta}\mathfrak{b}(\eta,y)(\eta-\xi_{T})+\mathfrak{b}(\eta,y)\right)

giving that

(3.164)

\frac{\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{b}^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta)}{\mathfrak{b}^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta)}=\frac{\partial_{\eta}\mathfrak{b}(\eta,y)}{\mathfrak{b}(\eta,y)^{2}}\left(\frac{\partial_{\eta}\mathfrak{b}(\eta,y)}{\mathfrak{b}(\eta,y)^{2}}\theta+1\right)^{-1}

and thus

(3.165)

\frac{1}{\theta-\theta_{+}}\leq\frac{\partial_{\theta}\mathfrak{b}^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta)}{\mathfrak{b}^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta)}\leq\frac{1}{\theta-\theta_{-}},

as required.

Finally, by definition of the local size
\mathbb{L}^{(u,1)}_{\Theta}(T)
we
dualize with
G\in C^{\infty}_{c}(T)
and obtain that

(3.166)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}F(\eta,y,t)G(\eta,y,t)t\delta\left(t-\mathfrak{b}_{E}(\eta,y)\right)\mathrm{d}\eta\mathrm{d}y\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}F\left(\eta,y,\mathfrak{b}(\eta,y)\right)G\left(\eta,y,{\mathfrak{b}_{E}}(\eta,y)\right)\mathfrak{b}_{E}(\eta,y)\mathrm{d}\eta\mathrm{d}y.

We enact the change of variables
\theta=\mathfrak{b}_{E}(\eta,y)(\eta-\xi_{T})
,
\zeta=\frac{y-x_{T}}{s_{T}}
with Jacobian it holds that

(3.167)

J(\eta,y)=\mathfrak{b}_{E}(\eta,y)\left(1+(\eta-\xi_{T})\frac{\partial_{\eta}\mathfrak{b}_{E}(\eta,y)}{\mathfrak{b}_{E}(\eta,y)}\right).

From bound (
3.150
) we obtain that when
(\eta,y,t)\in T
and, in particular,
t(\eta-\xi_{T})\in\Theta
it holds that

(3.168)

0<J(\eta,y)\lesssim 1

so the change of variables is valid. We set
J^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta)\coloneqq J(\eta(\theta),y(\zeta))
and from bound (
3.150
) we
obtain that

(3.169)

\mathrm{dist}\left(\theta,\mathbb{R}\setminus\Theta\right)\lesssim J^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta)\lesssim 1.

Rewriting (
3.166
) in new coordinates
we get

(3.170)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}F\left(\eta,y,\mathfrak{b}(\eta,y)\right)G\left(\eta,y,\mathfrak{b}(\eta,y)\right)\mathfrak{b}(\eta,y)\frac{\mathrm{d}\eta\mathrm{d}y}{|\Theta|s_{T}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}F\circ\pi_{T}\left(\theta,\zeta,\mathfrak{b}_{E}^{\ast}(\sigma,\zeta)\right)G\left(\theta,\zeta,\mathfrak{b}_{E}^{\ast}(\sigma,\zeta)\right)\frac{1}{J^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta)}\frac{\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}\zeta}{|\Theta|}.

Taking the upper bound over
G
with
\|G\circ\pi_{T}\|_{L^{u^{\prime}}_{\frac{\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}\zeta}{|\Theta|}}L^{\infty}_{\frac{\mathrm{d}\sigma}{\sigma}}}\leq 1
we get that

(3.171)

\displaystyle\left\|F(\eta,y,t)t\mathrm{d}_{t}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(\eta,y,t)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{(u,1)}_{(\Theta,\widetilde{\Theta})}(T)}^{u}=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}}\left|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathfrak{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}}(F\circ\pi_{T})\left(\theta,\zeta,\mathfrak{b}_{E}^{\ast}(\sigma,\zeta)\right)\right|^{u}J^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta)^{-u}\frac{\mathrm{d}\theta\mathrm{d}\zeta}{|\Theta|},

that shows (
3.154
). Finally, using the
inverse change of variables

(\eta,y)=\left(\xi_{T}+\theta\left(s_{T}\mathfrak{b}_{E}^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta)\right)^{-1},x_{T}+s_{T}\zeta\right)
we
get

(3.172)

\displaystyle\left\|F(\eta,y,t)t\mathrm{d}_{t}\mathbbm{1}_{E}(\eta,y,t)\right\|_{\mathbb{L}^{(u,1)}_{(\Theta,\widetilde{\Theta})}(T)}^{u}

\displaystyle=\begin{aligned} &\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{\pi_{T}\left(\mathfrak{T}_{\widetilde{\Theta}}\right)}\left(\eta,y,\mathfrak{b}_{E}(\eta,y)\right)\left|F\left(\eta,y,\mathfrak{b}_{E}(\eta,y)\right)\right|^{u}\\
&\qquad\times J(\eta,y)^{1-u}\mathfrak{b}_{E}(\eta,y)\frac{\mathrm{d}\eta\mathrm{d}y}{|\Theta|s_{T}}.\end{aligned}

This shows (
3.151
), concluding the proof.
∎

4.
The single tree estimate

In this section we focus on proving
Theorem

2
(bounds
(
1.25
) and (
1.26
)). These bounds correspond to the

L^{\infty}
endpoint bound when applying

Proposition

3.3
since, tautologically,

L_{\mu}^{\infty}\mathbb{S}\equiv\mathbb{S}
. We will use
Theorem

2
recurrently in

Section

5
to prove to prove bounds
(
1.17
) and
(
1.21
).

The bounds (
1.25
) and (
1.26
) are often referred to
as the single tree estimates since the sizes in play are generated using
identity (
3.67
) starting from local sizes indexed by
single trees. Accordingly, it is sufficient to fix
T\in\mathbb{T}_{\Theta}
and
show that that bounds

(4.1)

\displaystyle\|F_{1}F_{2}F_{3}\|_{\left(\mathbb{I}^{(l,l,l)}_{\Theta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}+\mathbb{I}^{(o,l,l)}_{\Theta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}\right)(T)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|F_{1}\|_{\mathbb{F}_{\Theta}^{u_{1}}\Phi_{2\mathfrak{r}}^{N-3}(T)}\|F_{2}\|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}_{\Gamma_{2}}^{u_{2}}\Phi_{2\mathfrak{r}}^{N-3}(T)}\|F_{3}\|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}_{\Gamma_{3}}^{u_{3}}\Phi_{2\mathfrak{r}}^{N-3}(T)},

(4.2)

\displaystyle\|F_{1}F_{2}F_{3}\|_{\left(\mathbb{I}^{(l,l,o)}_{\Theta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}+\mathbb{I}^{(l,o,l)}_{\Theta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}\right)(T)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|F_{1}\|_{\mathbb{F}_{\Theta}^{u_{1}}\Phi_{4\mathfrak{r}}^{N-5}(T)}\|F_{2}\|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}_{\Gamma_{2}}^{u_{2}}\Phi_{4\mathfrak{r}}^{N-5}(T)}\|F_{3}\|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}_{\Gamma_{3}}^{u_{3}}\Phi_{4\mathfrak{r}}^{N-5}(T)},

and

(4.3)

\|F_{1}F_{2}F_{3}\|_{\mathbb{I}^{(l,o,o)}_{\Theta}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}\lesssim\|F_{1}\|_{\mathbb{F}_{\Theta}^{u_{1}}\Phi_{4\mathfrak{r}}^{N-5}(T)}\|F_{2}F_{3}\|_{\widetilde{\mathbb{F}}_{\Gamma_{\times}}^{u_{\times}}(\Phi\otimes\Phi)_{4\mathfrak{r}}^{N-5}(T)}.

hold for all
F_{j}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(T){\otimes\Phi_{4\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}}^{\prime}.

Let us argue that this implies the claim of
Theorem

2
. Assuming that the
bounds (
4.1
), (
4.2
), and
(
4.3
) hold, using a standard mollification argument, the
same inequalities continue to hold for all functions
F_{j}\in L^{\infty}(T){\otimes\Phi_{4\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}}^{\prime}
that are compactly supported in the interior
of
T
. As a matter of fact all the sizes in play are lower-semicontinuous
when convolving with a compactly supported approximate identity. To
extend bounds (
4.1
), (
4.2
), and
(
4.3
) from the case of functions
F_{j}
that are compactly
supported in
T
to arbitrary functions
F_{j}\in L^{\infty}_{\operatorname{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}){\otimes\Phi_{4\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}}^{\prime}
let us set

(4.4)

\mathbb{K}_{n}=\begin{aligned} \Big(T\cap T_{\Theta}(\xi_{T}+2^{-n},x_{T},(1-2^{-n})&s_{T})\cap T_{\Theta}(\xi_{T}+2^{-n},x_{T},(1-2^{-n})s_{T})\Big)\\
&\setminus\{(\eta,y,t),t>2^{-n}s_{T}\}\end{aligned}

and let us assume bounds (
4.1
), (
4.2
),
and (
4.3
) hold for
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{K}_{n}}F_{j}
in place of

F_{j}
. According to
Definition

3.15
of sizes

\mathbb{I}_{\Theta}^{(\star_{1},\star_{2},\star_{3})}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}
, we have that

(4.5)

\displaystyle\|F_{1}F_{2}F_{3}\|_{\mathbb{I}_{\Theta}^{(\star_{1},\star_{2},\star_{3})}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}\leq\liminf_{n}\left\|\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{K}_{n}}F_{1}F_{2}F_{3}\right\|_{\mathbb{I}_{\Theta}^{(\star_{1},\star_{2},\star_{3})}\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}(T)}

for any
(\star_{1},\star_{2},\star_{3})\in\{o,l\}^{3}
. On the other hand,
according to (
3.67
), the terms on

\mathrm{RHS}{}
(
1.25
) and
\mathrm{RHS}{\eqref{eq:STE:l-o-o}}
increase at most
by a fixed factor when replacing
F_{j}
by
\mathbbm{1}_{W^{+}}{F_{j}}
or by
\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{R}^{3}_{+}\setminus W^{-}}F_{j}
with
W^{\pm}\in\mathbb{T}_{\Theta}^{\cup}
; the transformation

F_{j}\mapsto\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{K}_{n}}F_{j}
can be expressed as at most
4
such
steps. This concludes the reduction to
F_{j}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(T){\otimes\Phi_{4\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}}^{\prime}
.

Thus, for the subsequent proof we assume that
F_{j}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(T){\otimes\Phi_{4\mathfrak{r}}^{\infty}}^{\prime}
; let us introduce the notation

(4.6)

F_{j}^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma):=e^{-2\pi i\alpha_{j}\xi_{T}s_{T}\zeta}F_{j}\circ\pi_{T}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma),\qquad j\in\{1,2,3\};

According to (
1.11
) it holds that
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}=1+\alpha_{2}+\alpha_{3}=0
so

(4.7)

\Big(\prod_{j=1}^{3}F_{j}\Big)\circ\pi_{T}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)=\prod_{j=1}^{3}F_{j}^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma).

With the notation of
Definition

3.15
, given a wave packet
\varphi\in\Phi^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{r}/2}
we define the wave packets
\widetilde{\varphi}_{j,\theta}^{l}(z)
and
\widetilde{\varphi}_{j,\theta}^{o}(z)
to satisfy

(4.8)

\displaystyle(-d_{z}+2\pi i\theta_{\Gamma_{j}})\widetilde{\varphi}_{j,\theta}^{l}(z)=\varphi_{j,\theta}^{l}(z),

\displaystyle(-d_{z}+2\pi i\theta_{\Gamma_{j}})\widetilde{\varphi}_{j,\theta}^{o}(z)=z\varphi_{j,\theta}^{o}(z)

by setting

(4.9)

\widehat{\widetilde{\varphi}_{j,\theta}^{l}}\left(\widehat{z}\right)\coloneqq\frac{\widehat{\varphi_{j,\theta}^{l}}\left(\widehat{z}\right)}{-2\pi i\left(\widehat{z}-\theta_{\Gamma_{j}}\right)}.

and

(4.10)

\widehat{\widetilde{\varphi}_{j,\theta}^{o}}\left(\widehat{z}\right)\coloneqq\frac{\partial\widehat{\varphi_{j,\theta}^{o}}\left(\widehat{z}\right)}{-4\pi^{2}\left(\widehat{z}-\theta_{\Gamma_{j}}\right)}.

These expressions give well defined wave packets
\widetilde{\varphi}_{j,\theta}^{l},\widetilde{\varphi}_{j,\theta}^{o}\in\Phi^{\infty}_{\mathfrak{r}}
with

(4.11)

\|\widetilde{\varphi}_{j,\theta}^{o}\|_{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N-1}}+\|\widetilde{\varphi}_{j,\theta}^{l}\|_{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N-1}}\lesssim\|\varphi_{j,\theta}^{o}\|_{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}}+\|\varphi_{j,\theta}^{l}\|_{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}}^{N}}\|\varphi_{j}\|_{\Phi_{\mathfrak{r}/2}^{N}}.

This holds since
\widehat{\varphi_{j,\theta}^{l}}\left(\widehat{z}\right)=\widehat{\varphi_{j}}\left(\widehat{z}\right)-\widehat{\varphi_{j}}(\theta_{\Gamma_{j}})\widehat{\omega}\left(\frac{\widehat{z}}{\mathfrak{r}}\right)
and
\partial\widehat{\varphi_{j,\theta}^{o}}\left(\widehat{z}\right)=\widehat{\varphi}_{j}(\theta_{\Gamma_{j}})\mathfrak{r}^{-1}\widehat{\omega}^{\prime}\left(\frac{\widehat{z}}{\mathfrak{r}}\right)
and in particular,
\widehat{\varphi_{j,\theta}^{l}}(\theta_{\Gamma_{j}})=\mathrm{d}_{\widehat{z}}\widehat{\varphi_{j,\theta}^{o}}(\theta_{\Gamma_{j}})=0
.

Finally, we mention that main motivation for introducing the specific sizes in

Section

3.2
is given by the quantities arising in
the proof of
Theorem

2
.

We offer the following suggestions for a preliminary reading of these proofs.
First of all one can suppose that
T=T_{\Theta}(0,0,1)
. Next, we
encourage the reader to suppose that
F_{j}^{\ast}=\mathrm{Emb}[f_{j}]\circ\Gamma_{j}\circ\pi_{T}
. This guarantees that

(4.12)

\displaystyle F_{1}^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)[\varphi]\coloneqq f_{1}\ast\mathrm{Dil}_{\sigma}\operatorname{Mod}_{\theta}\varphi^{\vee},

\displaystyle F_{j}^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)[\varphi]\coloneqq f_{j}\ast\mathrm{Dil}_{\beta_{j}\sigma}\operatorname{Mod}_{\theta_{\Gamma_{j}}}\varphi^{\vee}.

where
\theta_{\Gamma_{j}}=\alpha_{j}\beta_{j}(\theta+\gamma_{j})
. Furthermore,
this assumption guarantees that

(4.13)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}_{\sigma}(\theta)F_{1}^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)[\varphi]=\sigma\mathrm{d}_{\sigma}\left(f_{1}\ast\mathrm{Dil}_{\sigma}\operatorname{Mod}_{\theta}\varphi^{\vee}\right),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}_{\sigma}(\theta_{\Gamma_{j}})F_{j}^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)[\varphi]=\sigma\mathrm{d}_{\sigma}\left(f_{j}\ast\mathrm{Dil}_{\beta_{j}\sigma}\operatorname{Mod}_{\theta_{\Gamma_{j}}}\varphi^{\vee}\right),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(\theta)F_{1}^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)[\varphi]=\sigma\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}\left(f_{1}\ast\mathrm{Dil}_{\sigma}\operatorname{Mod}_{\theta}\varphi^{\vee}\right),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}_{\zeta}(\theta_{\Gamma_{j}})F_{j}^{\ast}(\theta,\zeta,\sigma)[\varphi]=\beta_{j}\sigma\mathrm{d}_{\zeta}\left(f_{j}\ast\mathrm{Dil}_{\beta_{j}\sigma}\operatorname{Mod}_{\theta_{\Gamma_{j}}}\varphi^{\vee}\right).

In particular, any term in the subsequent manipulations that contains the
difference between the
\mathrm{LHS}{}
and
\mathrm{RHS}{}
of the quantities above vanishes.
In the complete proof the defect sizes included in
\mathbb{F}_{\Theta}^{u_{1}}\Phi^{N}_{\mathfrak{r}}
,
